The mconv.spacing program

Mconv.spacing removes as many as possible of the "false spaces" in the text resulting from Tokunaga's use of a single character to separate both words and compound-members. The corrections fall into two classes, which alternate throughout the (rather long) file: stem corrections (e.g. "always remove a space after ratna"), and compound corrections (e.g. "always remove a space between bhiima and forms of paraakrama-"). Mconv.spacing is automatically generated by two further programs, mkmconv and edmconv.

Note that the original program makes direct use of accented characters in the CSX encoding; here it has been converted to use Unicode equivalents to ensure readability on modern computers. This means that downloading and attempting to run the program as it stands is unlikely to work. 


#!/usr/bin/perl -p
#
study;
next if /^%/;

# These stem corrections generated by
# "mkmconv gītā.tokunaga.csx gītā.correct.csx"
# January 31, 1995: 3.27 p.m.

s/(\.|\s)([a'])kṣ([iy]) /$1$2kṣ$3./g;
s/(\.|\s)([a'])lpa /$1$2lpa./g;
s/(\.|\s)([a'])mb([uv]) /$1$2mb$3./g;
